**** TRANSIENT BUSINESS REGULATIONS ****
§ 110.01 DEFINITIONS.
   The singular shall include the plural; the plural shall include the
singular; and the masculine shall include the feminine and the neuter. For the
purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless the context
clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   LEGAL HOLIDAY. New Year’s Day, Memorial Day, Juneteenth, Independence Day,
Labor Day, Thanksgiving Day, and Christmas.
   PERSON. Any natural person, partnership, association, corporation, or other
legal entity.
   TRANSIENT RETAIL BUSINESS.
      (1)   Engaging in peddling, soliciting, or taking orders, either by
sample or otherwise, for any goods, wares, or merchandise upon any street,
alley, sidewalk, or public ground, or from house to house, within the borough;
or
      (2)   Selling, soliciting, or taking orders for any goods, wares, or
merchandise, from a fixed location within the borough, on a temporary basis,
which shall include, but not be limited to, such activities conducted at the
time of special occasions or celebrations, for seasonal purposes, or for or in
advance of specific yearly holidays.
(Prior Code, § 13, 101) (Ord. 227, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 110.02 LICENSE REQUIRED; CONDITIONS OF ISSUANCE; FEE.
   (A)   No person shall engage in any transient retail business within the
borough without first having obtained from the Mayor a license, for which a
fee, which shall be for the use of the borough, to be set, from time to time,
by resolution of the Borough Council, shall be charged.
   (B)   The transient business license fee is established by the Borough
Council to be $25 per day.
(Prior Code, § 13, 102) (Ord. 227, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992;
Res. 95-3-1, passed 3-13-1995) Penalty, see §
110.99
§ 110.03 EXCEPTIONS.
   (A)   No license fee shall be charged:
      (1)   To farmers selling their own produce;
      (2)   For the sale of goods, wares, and merchandise, donated by the
owners thereof, the proceeds whereof are to be applied to any charitable or
philanthropic purpose;
      (3)   To any manufacturer or producer in the sale of bread and bakery
products, meat and meat products, or milk and milk products;
      (4)   To children under the age of 18 years who take orders for and
deliver newspapers, greeting cards, candy, bakery products, and the like, or
who represent the Girl Scouts or Scouting America or similar organizations;
      (5)   To any honorable discharged member of any of the armed services who
complies with state law, hereafter amended, supplemented, modified, or
reenacted by the General Assembly of the commonwealth, and who procures from
the Prothonotary a certificate in pursuance of the Act of 1867;
      (6)   To the seeking or taking of orders by insurance agents or brokers
licensed under the insurance laws of the commonwealth;
      (7)   To any person who has complied with the provisions of the
Charitable Organization Reform Act, 10 P.S. §§ 162.1 et seq., as hereafter
amended, supplemented, modified, or reenacted by the General Assembly of the
commonwealth; or
      (8)   For taking orders for merchandise, by sample, from dealers or
merchants for individuals or companies who pay a license or business privilege
tax at their chief place of business.
   (B)   (1)   But, all persons exempted hereby from the payment of the license
fee shall be required to register with the Mayor and obtain a license without
fee; provided, any person dealing in one or more of the exempted categories
mentioned in division (A) above, and dealing with other goods, wares, or
merchandise not so exempted, shall be subject to the payment of the license fee
fixed by this subchapter for his or her activities in connection with the sale
of goods, wares, and merchandise not in such exempted categories.
      (2)   Provided further, the Mayor may similarly exempt from payment of
the license fee, but not from registering with him or her, persons working
without compensation and selling goods, wares, or merchandise for the sole
benefit of any nonprofit corporation. Provided further, every license issued
under the provisions of this subchapter shall be issued on an individual basis
to any person or persons engaging in such business.
      (3)   Every individual shall obtain a separate license, issued to him or
her in his or her name, and the license fee hereby imposed shall be applicable
to every such individual license, except that a representative of a charitable
organization may obtain licenses for the applicants therein.
(Prior Code, § 13, 103) (Ord. 227, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
110.99
§ 110.04 LICENSE APPLICATION.
   (A)   Every person desiring a license under this subchapter shall first make
application to the Mayor for such license. He or she shall, when making
application, exhibit a valid license from any state or county officer, if such
license is also required.
   (B)   The applicant shall state:
      (1)   His or her criminal record, if any;
      (2)   Name and address of the person by whom he or she is employed;
      (3)   Type of goods, wares, and merchandise he or she wishes to deal with
in such transient retail business;
      (4)   Length of time for which license is to be issued; and
      (5)   Type and license number of the vehicle to be used, if any.
(Prior Code, § 13, 104) (Ord. 227, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 110.05 ISSUANCE OF LICENSE; CUSTODY, DISPLAY, AND EXHIBIT.
   (A)   Upon receipt of such application and the prescribed fee, the Mayor, if
he or she shall find such application in order, shall issue the license
required under this subchapter. Such license shall contain the information
required to be given on the application therefor.
   (B)   (1)   Every license holder shall carry such license upon his or her
person if engaged in transient retail business from house to house or upon any
of the streets, alleys, sidewalks, or public grounds, or shall display such
license at the location where he or she shall engage in such business if doing
so at a fixed location.
      (2)   He or she shall exhibit such license, upon request, to all police
officers, municipal officials, and citizens or residents of the borough.
(Prior Code, § 13, 105) (Ord. 227, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
110.99
§ 110.06 PROHIBITED ACTS.
   No person in any transient retail business shall:
   (A)   Sell any product or type of product not mentioned in his or her
license;
   (B)   Hawk or cry his or her wares upon any of the streets, alleys,
sidewalks, or public grounds in the borough;
   (C)   When operating from a vehicle, stop or park such vehicle upon any of
the streets or alleys in the borough for longer than necessary in order to sell
therefrom to persons residing or working in the immediate vicinity;
   (D)   Park any vehicle upon any of the streets or alleys in the borough for
the purpose of sorting, rearranging, or cleaning any of his or her goods,
wares, or merchandise or of disposing of any carton, wrapping material, or
stock, wares, or foodstuffs which have become unsaleable through handling, age,
or otherwise; or
   (E)   Engage in any business activity, except by prior appointment, at any
time on a Sunday or legal holiday or at any time before 8:00 a.m. or after 6:00
p.m. on any day of the week other than a Sunday or legal holiday.
(Prior Code, § 13, 106) (Ord. 227, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
110.99
§ 110.07 SUPERVISION; RECORDS AND REPORTS.
   The Mayor shall supervise the activities of all persons holding licenses
under this subchapter. He or she shall keep a record of all licenses issued
hereunder and shall make a report thereof each month to the Borough Council.
(Prior Code, § 13, 107) (Ord. 227, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 110.08 SUSPENSION AND REVOCATION OF LICENSE; APPEAL.
   (A)   The Mayor is hereby authorized to suspend or revoke any license issued
under this subchapter when he or she deems such suspension or revocation to be
beneficial to the public health, safety, or morals, for violation of any
provision of this subchapter, or for giving false information upon any
application for a license hereunder.
   (B)   Appeals from any suspension or revocation may be made to the Borough
Council at any time within ten days after such suspension or revocation.
   (C)   No part of a license fee shall be refunded to any person whose license
shall have been suspended or revoked.
(Prior Code, § 13, 108) (Ord. 227, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 110.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person, firm, or corporation who shall violate any provision of §§
110.01 through
110.08 shall, upon conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine not more than
$600 and costs or, in default of payment, shall be subject to imprisonment for
not more than 30 days. Each day that a violation of §§
110.01 through
110.08 continues shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 13, 109) (Ord. 227, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 130.01 ALCOHOLIC BEVERAGES.
   (A)   Legislative findings. It is hereby found by the Borough Council that
the consumption of alcoholic beverages on or in public streets, sidewalks,
parks, alleys, or other public places constitutes a public nuisance and
threatens the public health, safety, and welfare. The purpose of this section
is to promote public safety and convenience, prevent accidents, reduce
littering, preserve the character of the borough and promote the general peace,
good order, comfort, and welfare of the borough inhabitants.
(Prior Code, § 6, 101)
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different
meaning.
      ALCOHOLIC BEVERAGE. Any liquor, spiritous, vinous, fermented, or
otherwise alcoholic beverage. The term includes any beer, lager beer, ale,
porter, or similar fermented beverage by whatsoever name such BEVERAGE may be
called.
      BOROUGH. The Borough of Everett, County of Bedford, Commonwealth of
Pennsylvania, with its administrative office at Mechanic Street, Everett,
Pennsylvania.
      SHALL. The act referred to is always mandatory and not merely directory.
(Prior Code, § 6, 102)
   (C)   Prohibited activities.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person, whether inside or outside of a
motor vehicle, to consume an alcoholic beverage or portion thereof on or in any
public street, sidewalk, park, alley, or other public place within the borough.
      (2)   It shall be unlawful for any person, whether inside or outside of a
motor vehicle, to transport, possess, or have physical control over an
alcoholic beverage in an open bottle, can, glass, or other container on or in
any public street, sidewalk, park, alley, or other public place within the
borough.
(Prior Code, § 6, 103)
(Ord. 265, passed 8-10-1987) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.02 DISORDERLY CONDUCT.
   (A)   Disorderly conduct, as defined in the Pennsylvania Crime Code of 1972,
18 Pa.C.S.A. § 5503, is hereby prohibited within the borough. A person is
guilty of disorderly conduct if he or she:
      (1)   Engage in fighting or in threatening, violent, or tumultuous
behavior;
      (2)   Make unreasonable noise; or
      (3)   Create a hazardous or physically offensive condition by any act
which serves no legitimate purpose of the actor.
   (B)   Provided, as used in this section, the word PUBLIC means affecting or
likely to affect persons in a place to which the public or a substantial group
has access. Among the places included are streets, alleys, and sidewalks,
transport facilities, schools, prisons, apartment houses, places of business or
amusement, any neighborhood, or any premises which are open to the public.
(Prior Code, § 6, 201) (Ord. 218, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.03 THROWING OF OBJECTS INTO STREETS.
   The throwing, kicking, or knocking of any ball, snowballs, stones, or any
other missile upon or into any of the public streets, alleys, or sidewalks in
the borough is hereby prohibited.
(Prior Code, § 6, 401) (Ord. 220, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
130.99
**** FIREARMS ****
§ 130.15 DISCHARGE OF FIREARMS PROHIBITED.
   Except in necessary defense of person and property and except as provided in
§
130.17, it shall be unlawful for any person to use, fire, or discharge any gun
or other firearm within the borough.
(Prior Code, § 6, 301) (Ord. 219, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.16 USE OF AIR RIFLES, BOW AND ARROWS, OR SIMILAR DEVICES RESTRICTED.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to discharge any air rifle, air pistol,
spring gun, spring pistol, BB gun, bow and arrow, or similar device, or any
implement that is not a firearm but which impels a pellet of any kind with a
force that can reasonably be expected to cause bodily harm, at any place within
the borough, except as provided in §
130.17, and except on a target range which is properly constructed to trap or
stop the projectile as ascertained by the Chief of Police.
(Prior Code, § 6, 302) (Ord. 219, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.17 EXCEPTIONS.
   This subchapter shall not apply to:
   (A)   Persons licensed to hunt in this commonwealth while actually engaged
in hunting where permitted under the laws of the commonwealth;
   (B)   Members of any organization incorporated under laws of this
commonwealth engaged in target shooting upon the grounds or property belonging
to or under the control of such organization; or
   (C)   Any law enforcement officers when used in the discharge of their
official duties.
(Prior Code, § 6, 303) (Ord. 219, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
**** CURFEW FOR MINORS ****
§ 130.30 DEFINITIONS AND INTERPRETATION.
   In this subchapter, the singular shall include the plural, the plural shall
include the singular, and the masculine shall include the feminine and the
neuter. For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   MINOR. Person under the age of 18 years.
   PARENT. Any natural parent of a minor, as herein defined, or a guardian, or
any adult person responsible for the care and custody of a minor. When used in
this subchapter, PARENT shall mean one or both parents.
   PUBLIC PLACE. Any public street, alley, sidewalk, park, playground, public
building, or vacant lot in the borough.
   REMAIN. To stay behind, to tarry, and to stay unnecessarily upon the
streets, including the congregating of groups (or of interacting minors)
totaling four or more persons in which any minor involved would not be using
the streets for emergencies or ordinary purposes such as mere passage of going
home.
(Prior Code, § 6, 601) (Ord. 222, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 130.31 PURPOSES.
   This is a curfew subchapter prescribing, in accordance with prevailing
community standards, regulations for the conduct of minors on streets at night,
for the protection of younger children in the borough from each other and from
other persons on the streets during nighttime hours, for the enforcement of
parental control and responsibility for their children, for the protection of
the public from nocturnal mischief by minors, and for the reduction of the
incident of juvenile criminal activity, all for the good of minors, for the
furtherance of family responsibility, and for the public good, safety, and
welfare.
(Prior Code, § 6, 602) (Ord. 222, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 130.32 CURFEW; EXCEPTIONS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any minor 15 years of age and under to be or
remain in or upon any public highway, park, or other public place within the
borough, or in any enclosure or vehicle which is on or in close proximity to
any such public place within the borough, between the hours of 10:00 p.m. and
6:00 a.m. on the following day, without being accompanied by an adult family
member. It shall be unlawful for any minor aged 16 to 17 years old to be or
remain on the same between the hours of 11:00 p.m. and 5:00 a.m. on the
following day, without being accompanied by an adult family member.
   (B)   Exceptions to division (A) above are the following:
      (1)   Minor accompanied by parent, guardian, or other person having legal
care or custody of such minor;
      (2)   Minor possessing a written statement dated that day and signed by
parent, guardian, or other person having the legal care or custody of such
minor, which statement specifies the time, place, purpose, and necessity of the
minor being in a public place contrary to this subchapter;
      (3)   Minor lawfully employed, making it necessary to be on or in
highways, streets, parks, and the like, as stated in division (A) above, and
possessing a current letter certifying the same and signed by employer, parent,
or guardian;
      (4)   Minor on an emergency errand; or
      (5)   Minor traveling to and from church, school, or municipal activity
with personal permission statement as in division (B)(2) above.
(Prior Code, § 6, 603) (Ord. 222, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992;
Ord. 2024-12-09, passed 12-9-2024) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.33 PARENTS NOT TO PERMIT VIOLATIONS.
   It is hereby made unlawful for any parent, guardian, or the person having
the legal care or custody of a minor to allow or permit such minor to violate
any of the provisions of this subchapter without legal justification therefor.
(Prior Code, § 6, 604) (Ord. 222, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-2991)
Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.34 PROCEDURE UPON VIOLATION.
   (A)   Any minor found upon the streets, alleys, parks, or public places
within the borough in violation of §
130.32 shall be taken into custody by the borough police or legally deputized
individual, be delivered to his or her parent(s), guardian, or person having
the legal custody of said minor, and be given a copy of this subchapter. A
report shall be filed and kept in a book for that specific purpose.
   (B)   If said parent, guardian, or person having the legal custody of said
minor shall again allow him or her to be on the streets, alleys, parks, or
public places in violation of §
130.32, said parent, guardian, or person having the legal custody of said minor
so offending shall, upon the second offense, be called along with the offender
and be so advised once again as to the penalty provisions contained in this
subchapter.
   (C)   Upon the third violation, said parent, guardian, or person will be
remanded to the District Justice for disposition.
(Prior Code, § 6, 605) (Ord. 222, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 130.35 PROCEDURE IN CASE OF REPEATED VIOLATIONS OR OTHER FACTORS INTERFERING
WITH ENFORCEMENT.
   Any minor who shall violate this subchapter more than three times may, at
the discretion of the proper borough officials, be reported to a society or
organization the purpose of which is to take charge of incorrigibles and
delinquents, and proceedings shall then be taken in the proper court of the
permanent welfare of such minor and a like procedure may be taken in cases
where the arrest of the parent is not effective, or where for any other reason,
the provisions of §
130.32 cannot be made effective by the imposition of fines and penalties.
(Prior Code, § 6, 607) (Ord. 222, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 130.36 POLICE DISCRETION IN AGE DETERMINATION.
   (A)   The police officers of the borough, in taking minors into custody,
shall use their discretion in determining age and, in doubtful cases, may
require positive proof of age.
   (B)   Until such age is furnished, the officer’s judgment shall prevail.
(Prior Code, § 6, 608) (Ord. 222, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §
130.01 shall, upon conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine not exceeding
$600 and costs, or, in default of payment thereof, shall be subject to
imprisonment for a term not to exceed 30 days.
(Prior Code, § 6, 104)
   (C)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §
130.02 shall, upon conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine of not more
than $600 plus costs and, in default of payment thereof, to a term of
imprisonment for a term not to exceed 30 days.
(Prior Code, § 6, 202)
   (D)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §
130.03 shall, upon conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine of not more
than $600 and costs, or in default of payment thereof, shall be subject to
imprisonment for a term not to exceed 30 days.
(Prior Code, § 6, 402)
   (E)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §§
130.15 through
130.17 shall, upon conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine of not more
than $600 and costs, or in default of payment thereof, shall be subject to
imprisonment for a term not to exceed 30 days. Each day that a violation of §§
130.15 through
130.17 continues shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 6, 304)
   (F)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §§
130.30 through
130.36 shall, upon conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine of not more
than $600 and costs, or, in default of payment thereof, shall be subject to
imprisonment for a term not to exceed 30 days.
(Prior Code, § 6, 606)
(Ord. 218, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 219, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 220, passed 4-7-
1969; Ord. 222, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 265, passed 8-10-1987; Ord. 271, passed
6-8-1992)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 150.001 DEFINITION AND INTERPRETATIONS.
   As used in this subchapter, the term PERSON shall include any individual,
corporation, partnership, association, firm, or other legal entity. The
singular shall include the plural, the plural shall include the singular, and
the masculine shall include the feminine and the neuter.
(Prior Code, § 6, 501) (Ord. 221, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 150.002 TAMPERING WITH PUBLIC PROPERTY ON STREETS, ALLEYS, OR PUBLIC GROUNDS
PROHIBITED.
   No person shall destroy, injure, tamper with, or deface any public property
of the borough, or any grass, walk, lamp, ornamental work, building, or street
light on or in any of the streets, alleys, sidewalks, or public grounds in the
borough.
(Prior Code, § 6, 502) (Ord. 221, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.003 TAMPERING WITH STAKES, POSTS, AND MONUMENTS PROHIBITED.
   No person shall, in any manner, interfere or meddle with, or pull, drive,
change, alter, or destroy any stake, post, monument, or other marking made,
placed, or set, or hereafter made, placed, or set, or caused to be done by the
authorities of the borough, to evidence the location, elevation, line, grade,
corner, or angle of any public street, alley, sidewalk, curb, gutter, drain, or
other public work, or thing.
(Prior Code, § 6, 503) (Ord. 221, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.004 TAMPERING WITH WARNING LAMPS, SIGNS, OR BARRICADES PROHIBITED.
   No person shall destroy, remove, deface, obliterate, or cover up any lamp,
sign, or barricade erected as a warning of danger by the authorities of the
borough or by any person doing work by permission of the authorities of the
borough on any of the streets, alleys, sidewalks, or bridges in the borough or
on any public grounds of the borough, within or without the borough.
(Prior Code, § 6, 504) (Ord. 221, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.005 REMOVAL OF MATERIAL FROM STREETS, ALLEYS, OR PUBLIC GROUNDS.
   No person shall take any earth, stone, or other material from any street,
alley, or public grounds in the borough.
(Prior Code, § 6, 505) (Ord. 221, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.006 DEPOSIT OF HARMFUL SUBSTANCES ON STREETS, ALLEYS, SIDEWALKS, OR
PUBLIC GROUNDS PROHIBITED.
   No person shall pour, throw, or deposit any harmful or destructive substance
or matter on any street, alley, sidewalk, or public grounds in the borough.
(Prior Code, § 6, 506) (Ord. 221, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.007 EXCEPTIONS.
   Sections
150.001 through
150.006 shall not apply to normal activities in connection with construction,
maintenance, and repair of streets, alleys, sidewalks, and public grounds and
the structures and fixtures located thereon or therein, or to incidental
results of work done thereon or therein upon permit from or by authority of the
borough.
(Prior Code, § 6, 507) (Ord. 221, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992)
§ 150.008 SNOW AND ICE REMOVAL FROM SIDEWALKS.
   (A)   Responsibility; time limit.
      (1)   The owner, occupant, or tenant of every property in the borough
fronting upon or alongside any of the streets in the borough is hereby required
to remove or cause to be removed from all of the sidewalks in front of or
alongside such property all snow and ice thereon fallen or formed, within 24
hours after the same shall have ceased to fall or to be formed. Provided, the
owner of a property shall be responsible for conforming to the requirements of
this section where such property is occupied by such owner or is vacant or
unoccupied, or where the property is a multiple-unit property, occupied by more
than one tenant or occupier. The tenant or occupier of a property shall be so
responsible where such property comprises a single unit, occupied by such
tenant or occupier only.
      (2)   It shall be unlawful and shall constitute a public nuisance for
anyone to throw, shovel, plow, or use a snow blower to deposit snow into or
upon any street or highway in the borough with the intent of allowing such snow
to remain on such streets and highways.
      (3)   It shall be required of all homes and businesses along Main Street
between N. Hopewell traffic light and N. Spring Street traffic light, to push
snow onto the brick pad for street crew’s cleanup.
      (4)   It shall be required of all homes and businesses on borough streets
to park to one side of the street when snow removal begins. The movement of
your car to cleared side once plowed to make a plowing of opposite side easier
to clear. On even-numbered days, park north side or west side. On odd-numbered
days, park south side or east side.
(Prior Code, § 21, 301)
   (B)   Authority for borough to remove at expense of defaulting owner,
occupant, or tenant. In any case where the owner, occupant, or tenant, as
aforesaid in division (A) above, shall fail, neglect, or refuse to comply with
any of the provisions of division (A) above within the time limit prescribed
therein, the borough authorities may proceed immediately to clear all snow and/
or ice from the sidewalk of such delinquent, and to collect the expenses
thereof, with any additional amount allowed by law, from such owner, occupant
or tenant, as the case may be, which may be in addition to any fine or penalty
imposed under §
150.999.
(Prior Code, § 21, 302)
(Ord. 232, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 2021-06-02, passed 6-14-2021) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.009 SIDEWALK OBSTRUCTIONS AND USES.
   (A)   Definitions and interpretations. The word PERSON, as used in this
section, shall mean and include any natural person, association, firm, or
corporation. The singular shall include the plural, the plural shall include
the singular, and the masculine shall include the feminine and the neuter.
(Prior Code, § 21, 401)
   (B)   Prohibited acts.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person to:
         (a)   Place or maintain on any sidewalk in the borough any display of
merchandise except so as to allow a clear unobstructed width of five feet on
Main Street, four feet on every other street in the borough, for the use of
pedestrian traffic;
         (b)   Place or allow to remain on any sidewalk in the borough, except
temporarily during loading or unloading and pending immediate removal
therefrom, any goods, wares, merchandise, or any bulky substance or material
whatsoever; or
         (c)   To place or permit to remain any grass clippings upon any
sidewalk located in the borough.
      (2)   Provided, nothing herein shall prevent the holding by the merchants
of the borough for special “sidewalk sales,” when the same shall be
specifically authorized by the Mayor, and then when conducted subject to the
conditions prescribed by the Mayor for use and occupancy of the sidewalks.
(Prior Code, § 21, 402)
(Ord. 231, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 2022-07-02, passed 7-11-2022; Ord. 2025-05-13,
passed 5-12-2025) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.010 GRASS CLIPPINGS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to place or permit to be placed or
remain any grass clippings upon any street or highway located within the
borough.
(Ord. 2022-07-01, passed 7-11-2022) Penalty, see §
150.999
**** STREETS ****
§ 150.025 PERMIT REQUIRED TO EXCAVATE IN STREET OR ALLEY; FEE AND DEPOSIT OR
BOND.
   (A)   No person or persons, partnership, or corporation shall go upon the
streets, alleys, or highways within the borough to make ditches, trenches, or
any manner of excavation for the purpose of laying or repairing any manner of
water pipes, sewer pipes, electric, telegraph, or telephone lines or other
purposes, unless such person or persons, partnership, or corporation shall
first have procured from the Borough Council a permit, by paying a fee
established from time to time by resolution and shall also have deposited with
the Borough Secretary a sum equal to the prevailing price for replacement of
the street surface to be disturbed, as security for refilling such ditch,
trench, or other excavation, and placing the street, alley, or highway in as
good condition as it was prior to the digging or excavation herein referred to.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of the Borough Secretary to pay such deposit over
to the Borough Treasurer to be held by him or her until the Street Commissioner
or Street Committee shall first have approved in writing the refilling of such
ditch, trench or other excavation, when, if approved, such deposit, by proper
order of the Borough Council, shall be refunded to the party making the same.
Provided, in lieu of making such individual deposits, utilities may file a bond
with the borough in the amount fixed by the Borough Council.
(Prior Code, § 21, 101) (Ord. passed 4-5-1937; Ord. 202, passed 4-7-1969; Ord.
271, passed 6-8-1992) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.026 USE OF DEPOSIT.
   In the event that the refilling of said ditches, trenches, or excavation is
not approved by the Street Commissioner or Committee, then all or such part of
said sum as may be necessary for placing said street, alley, or highway in as
good condition as before shall be forfeited for said purpose, and the balance,
if any, refunded to the depositor.
(Prior Code, § 21, 102) (Ord. passed 4-5-1937)
§ 150.027 MANNER OF REFILLING AND RESURFACING.
   (A)   When a trench is opened across any street or alley, all precautions
must be taken that may be necessary in the matter of refilling the trench so as
to prevent future settling. The refilling to be done in six-inch layers well
tamped, and when such work is done on a paved street or macadamized street,
care must be taken in order to have the new paving over the trench the same
grade as originally existed.
   (B)   To accomplish this, the refilling of the trench shall be well tamped
in six-inch layers to a point about eighteen inches below surface of street or
alley; from this point upward to the bottom of material of improved street the
trench must be widened nine inches on each side, but the surface of the street
must not be disturbed for a width greater than the width of the trench below
the enlarged section. Into this enlarged section of trench shall be placed the
concrete of proper mixture, not less than six inches thick, and upon top of
same the roadway shall be replaced to correspond to the existing construction.
The width of such trenches not to exceed two feet or less if possible.
(Prior Code, § 21, 103) (Ord. passed 4-5-1937) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.028 EXCAVATIONS IN STATE HIGHWAYS.
   All work referred to in this subchapter done in any street in the borough
that is a state highway shall not be subject to this subchapter, but instead
shall be governed by the provisions of the law relating to openings in the
surface of highways maintained by the state in boroughs.
(Prior Code, § 21, 104) (Ord. passed 4-5-1937; Ord. 202, passed 4-7-1969)
§ 150.029 DISPLAY OF RED LIGHTS REQUIRED.
   Red lights must be displayed from sunset to sunrise at all points of work
where there may be danger to the traveling public.
(Prior Code, § 21, 105) (Ord. passed 4-5-1937) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.030 REMOVAL OF EXCESS MATERIAL.
   All excess materials from excavations, from trenches and pavement
construction, and other materials must be properly and completely removed from
streets and alleys.
(Prior Code, § 21, 106) (Ord. passed 4-5-1937) Penalty, see §
150.999
**** SIDEWALK AND CURB CONSTRUCTION AND REPAIR ****
§ 150.045 PROPERTY OWNERS TO CONSTRUCT OR RECONSTRUCT SIDEWALK AND/OR CURB ON
NOTICE.
   Every owner of property in the borough shall, on 30 days’ notice from the
Borough Council, construct or reconstruct a sidewalk and/or curb, which shall
conform to all applicable requirements, in front of or alongside such property.
(Prior Code, § 21, 201) (Ord. 230, passed 4-7-1969) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.046 PROPERTY OWNERS TO REPAIR SIDEWALK AND/OR CURB ON NOTICE.
   Every owner of property in the borough shall, on ten days’ notice from the
Borough Council, repair the sidewalk and/or curb, in the manner stipulated in
such notice, in front of or alongside such property.
(Prior Code, § 21, 202) (Ord. 230, passed 4-7-1969) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.047 MATERIAL FOR SIDEWALKS AND CURBS.
   (A)   All sidewalks and curbs shall be constructed, reconstructed, or
repaired of concrete only, according to specifications adopted from time to
time by the Borough Council.
   (B)   Provided, where an existing sidewalk or curb is of a material other
than concrete, which was permissible for use at the time such sidewalk or curb
was constructed, such sidewalk or curb may be repaired with the same material
if at least two-thirds of such sidewalk or curb, as the case may be, in front
or along any property, is in good repair.
(Prior Code, § 21, 203) (Ord. 230, passed 4-7-1969) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.048 SIDEWALKS AND CURBS TO CONFORM TO GRADE AND LINE.
   (A)   All sidewalks and curbs shall conform to the established grade and
line thereof.
   (B)   Provided, where curb cuts shall be required for the purpose of
driveway entrances, such curb cuts and driveway entrance construction shall
conform to the requirements of the Pennsylvania Department of Transportation
that shall apply to state highways, except that where such curb cuts an
driveway entrances shall be along streets other than state highways, the
Borough Council shall have authority to grant variances where it shall deem the
same necessary because of special conditions.
(Prior Code, § 21, 204) (Ord. 230, passed 4-7-1969) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.049 SIDEWALK WIDTH; DEPTH OF CURBS.
   (A)   The paved portion of the sidewalk shall be at least four feet wide on
every street in the borough except:
      (1)   Main Street, where the paved portion thereof shall be seven feet
wide;
      (2)   Spring Street from Main Street to Fifth Street, where the paved
portion thereof shall be six feet wide; and
      (3)   Third Street, where the paved portion thereof shall be six feet
wide.
   (B)   The curbing shall be at least 18 inches deep on all streets.
   (C)   The depth of the sidewalk must be at least four inches thick.
(Prior Code, § 21, 205) (Ord. 230, passed 4-7-1969) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.050 DETERMINATION OF NECESSITY FOR WORK; INSPECTIONS; NOTIFICATION OF
COMPLETION.
   (A)   (1)   It shall be the duty of the Street Commissioner to determine, in
the case of any individual property, whether or not the sidewalk and/or curb
shall be reconstructed or repaired, and, if so, the specific part or parts
thereof to be constructed or repaired.
      (2)   The Street Commissioner may, at any time during the course of work
of constructing, reconstructing, or repairing any sidewalk or curb, visit the
site of such work in order to ascertain whether such work is being done
according to requirements, and he or she, or one of his or her assistants,
shall visit any such site for such purpose whenever requested by the property
owner.
   (B)   Within two days after completion of the work of construction,
reconstruction, or repair of any sidewalk and/or curb, it shall be the duty of
the owner of the property where such work was done to notify the Street
Commissioner of that fact, so that he or she may inspect such sidewalk and/or
curb to determine whether the same shall have been constructed, reconstructed,
or repaired in conformity with all applicable requirements.
(Prior Code, § 21, 206) (Ord. 230, passed 4-7-1969) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.051 AUTHORITY FOR PROPERTY OWNER TO DO WORK ON OWN INITIATIVE.
   Any property owner, upon his or her own initiative, and without notice from
any borough authority, may construct, reconstruct, or repair a sidewalk and/or
curb in front of or alongside his or her property, provided that such owner
shall first make application to the Borough Engineer and shall conform to the
requirements of this subchapter as to line and grade and as to material used,
and that he or she shall also notify the Street Commissioner, as required by §
150.050, within two days after completion of the work.
(Prior Code, § 21, 207) (Ord. 230, passed 4-7-1969)
§ 150.052 AUTHORITY FOR BOROUGH TO DO WORK AND COLLECT COST.
   Upon the neglect of any property owner to comply with any notice given as
provided in this subchapter, within the time limit stated therein, the borough
may proceed to construct, reconstruct, or repair the sidewalk and/or curb, as
the case may be, and may thereupon collect the cost thereof, with an additional
amount of 10% from such owner, in the manner permitted by law.
(Prior Code, § 21, 208) (Ord. 230, passed 4-7-1969)
§ 150.053 INSPECTION PRIOR TO TRANSFER.
   (A)   The Borough Council shall be notified of any transfer of title or
interest in property which abuts any street, alley, public way, or state
highway within the borough. Upon receipt of such notice, the Borough Council or
its duly authorized representative shall inspect the property and any existing
sidewalks or curbs and either issue a written certification of compliance with
the terms of this subchapter or a notice to construct, repair, or reconstruct
in accordance with §§
150.046 through
150.050.
   (B)   Such notice to the Borough Council of transfer of title or interest
shall be given as follows.
      (1)   At least ten days prior to transfer by deed, the borough shall
receive written notice of the proposed transfer.
      (2)   Both the transferor and the transferee shall be responsible for
notifying the Borough Council of the proposed transfer of title. Notice may be
given by either the transferor, the transferee, or by the authorized agent of
either the transferor or the transferee.
      (3)   In the event that the owner of a property is a corporation, the
directors, officers, and shareholders of the corporation shall notify the
Borough Council within 30 days after any transfer of ownership of stock in the
corporation which, when added with any other transfers of stock within a 12-
month period, constitutes a transfer of 50% or more of the issued and
outstanding share of the corporation.
      (4)   In the event that the owner of a property is a partnership, whether
general or limited, the partners shall notify the Borough Council within 30
days after any change of partnership interest, which when added with any other
change of partnership interest within a 12-month period, constitutes a transfer
of 50% or more of the ownership of the partnership.
(Prior Code, § 21, 209) (Ord. 2001-02-01, passed 2-12-2001) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.054 INSPECTION PRIOR TO ISSUANCE OF BUILDING PERMIT.
   Upon application for a building permit for any property which abuts any
street, alley, public way, or state highway within the borough, the Borough
Council or its authorized representative shall inspect the property and any
existing sidewalks or curbs and either issue a written certification of
compliance or a notice to construct, repair, or reconstruct in accordance with
§§
150.046 through
150.050.
(Prior Code, § 21, 210) (Ord. 2001-02-01, passed 2-12-2001)
**** MUNICIPAL TREES ****
§ 150.065 SHORT TITLE.
   This subchapter shall be known and may be cited as the “Municipal Tree
Ordinance of the Borough of Everett, County of Bedford, Commonwealth of
Pennsylvania.”
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024)
§ 150.066 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning. When not
inconsistent with the context, words used in the present tense include the
future, words in the plural include the singular, and words in the singular
include the plural. The word SHALL is mandatory and not merely directory.
   LARGE TREES. Those expected to attain a height in excess of 45 feet at
maturity.
   MEDIUM TREES. Those expected to attain a height of 30 feet to 45 feet at
maturity.
   MUNICIPALITY. The Borough of Everett, County of Bedford, Commonwealth of
Pennsylvania.
   PARK. Public parks.
   PERSON. Any person, firm, partnership, association, corporation, company, or
organization of any kind.
   PROPERTY LINE. The outer edge of a street or highway right-of-way.
   PUBLIC AREAS. All streets and parks as well as all other property owned by
the borough which are located in the borough.
   PUBLIC TREES. All shade and ornamental trees now and hereafter growing on
any public areas or where otherwise indicated.
   SMALL TREES. Those expected to attain a height of 20 feet to 30 feet at
maturity.
   STREET or HIGHWAY. The entire width of every public way or right-of-way when
any part thereof is open to the use of the public, as a matter of right, for
purposes of vehicular and pedestrian traffic.
   STREET TREES. All shade and ornamental trees now or hereafter growing on any
public street.
   TREE LAWN. The part of a street or highway not covered by sidewalk or other
paving, lying between the property line and that portion of the street or
highway usually used for vehicular traffic.
   TREE TOPPING. The severe cutting back of limits to stubs larger than three
inches in diameter within the tree’s crown to such a degree so as to remove the
normal canopy and disfigure the tree.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024)
§ 150.067 GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS.
   (A)   A no-fee borough notification permit is required in a public right-of-
way.
   (B)   No person shall plant, spray, fertilize, preserve, prune, remove, cut,
or otherwise disturb any public tree without first filing an application and
procuring a permit from the office of the borough. The person receiving the
permit shall abide by the specifications contained in this subchapter.
   (C)   Application for notification permits must be made at the office of the
borough not less than 48 hours in advance of the time the work is to be done.
   (D)   The office of the borough shall issue the notification permit provided
for herein if, in its judgment, the proposed work is desirable and the proposed
method and workmanship thereof are of a satisfactory nature. Any permit granted
shall contain a definite date of expiration, and the work shall be completed in
the time allowed on the notification permit and in the manner as therein
described. Any notification permit shall be void if its terms are violated.
   (E)   Notice of completion shall be given within five days to the office of
the borough for its inspection.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.068 PLANTING.
   (A)   The application required herein shall state:
      (1)   The number of trees to be set out;
      (2)   The location, grade, species, cultivar, or variety of each tree;
      (3)   The method of planting; and
      (4)   Such other information as the Council of the borough shall find
reasonably necessary to make a fair determination of whether a permit should be
issued.
   (B)   Whenever any tree shall be planted or set out in conflict with the
provisions of this section, the Council of the borough shall so notify the
property owner either personally or by certified mail and may direct the
property owner to remove the same. If the property owner shall fail to remove
the same within five days of the date of the notice, it shall be lawful for the
Council of the borough to remove or cause removal of the same, and the cost
thereof plus 10% shall be assessed to the owner as provided by law in the case
of special assessments or municipal claims.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.069 MAINTENANCE OF PROPERTY OWNER’S REQUEST.
   (A)   The application required herein shall state:
      (1)   The number of trees to be sprayed, fertilized, pruned, or otherwise
preserved;
      (2)   The kind of treatment to be administered;
      (3)   The composition of the spray material to be applied; and
      (4)   Such other information as the Council shall find reasonably
necessary to make a fair determination of whether a permit should be issued.
   (B)   No concrete or other material which could create a hazard when cutting
or trimming a tree may be utilized to repair a wound in said tree.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.070 REMOVAL, REPLANTING, AND REPLACEMENT.
   (A)   Wherever it is necessary to remove a tree or trees from a tree lawn in
connection with the paving of sidewalk or the paving or widening of the portion
of a street or highway used for vehicular traffic by the borough, the borough
shall replant such trees or replace them. Provided that conditions prevent
planting on tree lawns, this requirement will be satisfied if any equivalent
number of trees of the same size at maturity and species as provided for in
this subchapter are planted in an attractive manner on the adjoining property.
   (B)   (1)   No person or property owner shall remove a tree from the tree
lawn for the purpose of construction, or for any other reason, without first
filing an application and procuring a permit from the Council, and without
replacing the removed tree or trees.
      (2)   Such replacement shall meet the standards of size, species, and
placement as provided for in a permit. The person or property owner shall bear
the cost of removal and replacement of all trees removed.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.071 HAZARDOUS, DANGEROUS, OBSTRUCTING, AND/OR DISEASED TREES.
   (A)   (1)   It shall be the duty of any person or persons owning or
occupying real property bordering on any street upon which property there may
be trees located within or projecting within said street, to prune such trees
in such manner that they will not contain hazardous limbs, obstruct, or shade
the street lights, obstruct the passage of pedestrians on sidewalks, obstruct
vision of traffic signs, or obstruct view of any street or alley intersection.
The minimum clearance of any overhanging portion thereof shall be ten feet over
sidewalks and 16 feet over all streets.
      (2)   It shall be the duty of any person or persons owning or occupying
real property bordering on any street upon which property there may be trees
located within or projecting within said street to remove such hazardous or
dangerous streets.
      (3)   It shall be the duty of any person or persons owning or occupying
real property in the borough upon which property there may be trees to remove
said trees or parts thereof should they, in the opinion of the Council,
constitute a potential threat to other trees within the borough due to their
harboring of insects or disease.
   (B)   Should any person or persons owning real property within the borough
fail to prune/remove trees as provided in division (A) above, the Council shall
order such person or persons, within 30 days after receipt of written notice,
to so prune/remove such trees.
   (C)   The order required herein shall be served by mailing a copy of the
order to the last known address of the property owner, by certified mail.
   (D)   When a person to whom an order is directed shall fail to comply within
the specified time, it shall be lawful for the borough to prune/remove such
trees, and the cost thereof plus 10% shall be assessed to the property owner
for each time that the borough or its agents must conduct the work.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.072 ABUSE OR MUTILATION OF PUBLIC TREES.
   (A)   Unless specifically authorized by the office of the borough, no person
shall intentionally damage, cut, carve, transplant, or remove any tree; attach
any rope, wire, nails, advertising posters, or other contrivance to any tree;
allow any gaseous, liquid, or solid substance which is harmful to such trees to
come in contact with them; or set fire or permit any fire to burn when such
fire or the heat thereof will injure any portion of any tree.
   (B)   (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons to top any public
tree.
      (2)   Trees severely damaged by storms or other causes, or certain trees
under utility wires or other obstructions where other pruning practices are
impractical, may be exempted from this division (B) at the determination of the
Council.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.073 PROTECTION OF TREES.
   (A)   All public trees near any excavation or construction of any building,
structure, or street work shall be guarded with a good substantial fence,
frame, or box not less than four feet high and eight feet square, or at a
distance in feet from the tree equal to the diameter of the trunk in inches
D.B.H. (diameter at breast height), whichever is greater, and all building
material, dirt, or other debris shall be kept outside the barrier.
   (B)   No person shall excavate any ditches, tunnels, trenches, or lay any
drive within a radius of 15 feet from any public street without first obtaining
a written permit from the Commission.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.074 PLACING MATERIALS ON PUBLIC PROPERTY.
   No person shall deposit, place, store, or maintain upon any public area of
the borough, any stone, brick, sand, concrete, or other materials which may
impede the free passage of water, air, and fertilizer to the roots of any tree
growing therein, except by written permit of the office of the borough.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-9-2024) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.075 INTERFERENCE WITH THE OFFICE OF THE BOROUGH.
   No person shall hinder, prevent, delay, or interfere with the Council or any
of its assistants while engaged in carrying out the execution or enforcement of
this subchapter, regulations, and the like; provided, however, that nothing
herein shall be construed as an attempt to prohibit the pursuit of any remedy,
legal or equitable, in any court of competent jurisdiction for the protection
of property rights by the owner of any property within the borough.
(Ord. 12-09-24, passed 12-92024) Penalty, see §
150.999
§ 150.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §§
150.001 through
150.007 shall, upon conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine of not more
than $600 and costs, or in default of payment thereof, shall be subject to
imprisonment for a term not to exceed 30 days. Every day that a violation of §§
150.001 through
150.007 shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 5, 508)
   (C)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §
150.008 shall, upon conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine not
exceeding $600 and costs, or in default of payment thereof, shall be subject to
imprisonment for a term not to exceed 30 days. Each day that a violation of §
150.008 continues shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 21, 303)
   (D)   Any person who violates any provision of §
150.009(B)(1)(c) shall receive one written warning. At the second violation,
upon conviction, said person shall be sentenced to pay a fine of $50 and costs.
A third violation shall be $100 and costs and will increase in $50 increments
for further violations.
   (E)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §§
150.025 through
150.030 shall, upon conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine not
exceeding $600 and costs, or in default of payment thereof, shall be subject to
imprisonment for a term not to exceed 30 days. Each day that a violation of §§
150.025 through
150.030 continues shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 21, 107)
   (F)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §§
150.045 through
150.054 requiring notice to be given to the Borough Council shall, upon
conviction thereof, be sentenced to pay a fine not exceeding $600 and costs, or
in default of payment thereof, shall be subject to imprisonment for a term not
to exceed 30 days. Each day that a violation of §§
150.045 through
150.054 shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 21, 211)
   (G)   Any person who fails to comply with any or all of the requirements or
provisions of §§
150.065 through
150.075, or who fails or refuses to comply with any notice, order, or direction
of the Borough Council or the borough’s authorized agent shall be guilty of an
offense and, upon conviction, shall pay a fine to the borough of not less than
$25, nor more than $600, plus costs of prosecution.
(Ord. passed 4-5-1937; Ord. 202, passed 4-7-1969; Ord. 221, passed 4-7-1969;
Ord. 271, passed 6-8-1992; Ord. 2001-02-01, passed 2-12-2001; Ord. 12-09-24,
passed 12-9-2024; Ord. 2025-05-12, passed 5-12-2025; Ord. 2025-05-13, passed 5-
12-2025)
